    46	It must remain exact and checkable. For `epsilon={"var":"c"}` on c=0..1,
    47	c=0 is exact; c=1 needs a realised model for independent verification.
    48	Expression syntax alone cannot determine that requirement.
    49	
    50	The channel-admission counterexample has Kraus operators
    51	`K0=X/sqrt(2), K1=ZX/sqrt(2)`. Their squared operators sum to identity,
    52	but the channel maps |+><+| to I/2. A unitary preserves purity, so no
    53	logical unitary represents this map. An explicit controlled dilation
    54	does not make the reduced map unitary or supply an operator-norm epsilon
    55	for it. In contrast, two Kraus operators both X/sqrt(2) represent Ad_X;
    56	that representation is usable if it also binds phase-bearing U=X and
    57	its actual controlled model.
    58	
    59	## Recommended exact spec changes (§8.3)
    60	
    61	Add `realisation_model?` with initial format
    62	`bennett.native-model/1`. Extend the target schema with the following
    63	rules; retain §§4.4/8.1 matching and §12.3's controlled numerical claims.
    64	
    65	### Model requirements depend on evaluated epsilon
    66	
    67	```text
    68	Resolve the matched entry's k, c, requested eps and parameter bindings
    69	using §8.1 before determining model requirements. At each resolved
    70	context, if achieved epsilon evaluates exactly to zero, an omitted model
    71	denotes precisely the §4.4 opcode, including global phase and the exact
    72	controlled form. Positive evaluated epsilon requires a versioned
    73	realisation_model to discharge independent realised-operation
    74	verification. A model-complete target supplies one for every admitted
    75	context where its epsilon can be positive. Expression syntax alone
    76	neither requires nor exempts a model.
    77	
    78	Any supplied model MUST agree with the exact §4.4 operation in every
    79	zero-epsilon context and satisfy the advertised operator-norm bound
    80	otherwise. Zero-valued expressions independent of eps retain §17 item 9's
    81	exact matching convention. Existing legality rules for eps-dependent
    82	expressions, log2inv_eps, eps_decade and target matching remain unchanged;
    83	this does not make an otherwise illegal zero-epsilon expression legal.
    84	```
    85	
    86	### Native operation and error metric stay unitary
    87	
    88	```text
    89	Every native realisation model MUST identify a phase-bearing logical
    90	unitary U for each resolved parameter/control context. Native epsilon
    91	bounds ||U-V||op for the corresponding phase-fixed ideal §4.4 operation V.
    92	Normalization and model identity alone do not establish accuracy, an
    93	inverse, or phase-free permutation status.
    94	
    95	The native model kind is unitary. Matrix, Kraus or instrument data may
    96	be used as a representation only when their reduced logical map is
    97	rho -> U rho U-dagger, and the phase-bearing U is supplied as part of
    98	the same model. Kraus/instrument data alone are insufficient. Check
    99	complete-positivity/trace preservation when applicable and equivalence
   100	to the bound U on every matrix unit (or a checked symbolic proof of that
   101	identity), as well as U-dagger U=I. Thus a CPTP map that mixes a pure
   102	logical state is not a conformant native-unitary gate model under §4.4.
   103	
   104	For every admitted total control count the model identifies the actual
   105	controlled unitary, including phase relative to disabled branches.
   106	Exact controlled-U construction is allowed when explicitly bound as
   107	that actual implementation. Arbitrarily controlling a Kraus list does
   108	not define it. Instrument/environment conventions, when present, must
   109	reduce to the bound logical unitary in that control context.
   110	
   111	Channel claims compare induced channels; they do not replace the
   112	phase-bearing unitary, change epsilon to diamond error, or discharge
   113	§12.3's required numerical claim for controlled unitary variants.
   114	General logical measurement retains its existing CC/other published
   115	mechanisms; stochastic hardware failures remain separate target-model
   116	work. This repair makes no new nonunitary-native admission rule.
   117	```
   118	
   119	### Representation and independent reconstruction
   120	
   121	```text
   122	realisation_model has {version,kind,arity,basis,parameter_bindings,
   123	control_models,data}. Its canonical target bytes enter target_hash.
   124	After resolution, arity and basis determine the complete ordered logical
   125	operand bits. Explicit control models give the full context matrix;
   126	a controlled_U rule instead gives a base matrix and constructs identity
   127	on disabled branches and that matrix on the all-enabled branch, retaining
   128	its global phase. In that form arity/basis describe the base matrix and
   129	control_models adds the resolved c control wires. All matrix dimensions
   130	must match the resolved basis. No environment is silently counted as an
   131	extra logical input or discarded from a claimed unitary interface.
   132	
   133	parameter_bindings uses the same resolved k, c and eps as cost/error
   134	matching and the exact angle binding (§3.1 and §11.1 K20). A finite
   135	binary64 theta denotes exactly that binary rational, not a rounded
   136	source decimal. An explicit operand-to-wire permutation maps the entry's
   137	own-controls-then-effective-controls order (§8.3) to §12.1's allocation
   138	basis, inherited controls first. Both data and control models use this
   139	map; wrong parameter, order, dimensions or context bindings reject.
   140	
   141	Complex matrix entries are [real,imaginary] exact expressions over
   142	rational constants, i, pi, sqrt, exp, sin and cos with finite expression
   143	trees; rational constants are [numerator,positive_denominator] canonical
   144	decimal-string pairs in reduced form. Expression nodes are
   145	{op,args}; variables name only the declared parameter/context bindings.
   146	Data may additionally supply closed rational interval enclosures of an
   147	identified exact operation, with independently checkable enclosure and
   148	unitarity certificates. An unverified box alone is not an operation.
   149	
   150	The checker independently constructs U from the exact model in each
   151	invocation context, checks its normalization/control/accuracy obligations,
   152	and applies §12.1's interval numerical/channel procedures to requested
   153	claims. Inconclusive interval tests do not pass. The advertised epsilon
   154	remains a separate matching/error obligation, never the operator's
   155	definition. An absent approximate model never licenses ideal self-comparison.
   156	```
   157	
   158	For X, a concrete base model is below. Its rational/imaginary pairs are
   159	exact; changing the two real off-diagonal numerators from "1" to "-1"
   160	produces the −X model. `controlled_U` binds its actual controlled
   161	implementation for every admitted c, not just equality of uncontrolled
   162	channels. For non-parametric X, k=0, and eps is the requested share when
   163	bound; a zero-epsilon context needs no eps-dependent model expression.
   164	
   165	```json
   166	{
   167	  "version":"bennett.native-model/1",
   168	  "kind":"unitary",
   169	  "arity":1,
   170	  "basis":["t"],
   171	  "parameter_bindings":{"k":"k","c":"c"},
   172	  "control_models":{"kind":"controlled_U","controls":"c","disabled":"identity"},
   173	  "data":{"matrix":[
   174	    [[["0","1"],["0","1"]],[["1","1"],["0","1"]]],
   175	    [[["1","1"],["0","1"]],[["0","1"],["0","1"]]]
   176	  ]}
   177	}
   178	```
   179	
   180	Model-format version 1's default evaluation is exact expressions plus
   181	§12.1 interval enclosure, without any dimensional cap. Larger matrices
   182	may be generated symbolically from the same bindings rather than fully
   183	materialized; finite host checking limits yield unchecked obligations,
   184	not smaller supported algorithm domains.
   185	
   186	## Migration, hashes and publication
   187	
   188	Legacy targets without required models keep parse/execution compatibility.
   189	At zero evaluated epsilon use the exact-opcode convention. At positive
   190	epsilon, missing-model reporting leaves only the affected independent
   191	numerical/channel obligations unchecked; it cannot yield a checked
   192	executable certificate covering them. Unknown versions receive
   193	unsupported-model reporting, never a guessed operation. A supplied model
   194	that contradicts a zero-epsilon contract rejects that target claim.
   195	
   196	Recommend an authorized 0.9 K repair with this versioned target extension.
   197	Model bytes change target_hash and require regenerating every dependent

import json, hashlib, copy

//! Certificate schemas, bindings and references (§9.4, §12).
use crate::{
    err,
    json::Json,
    metadata::{hash, text, variant},
    sha256_hex, Error, Function, Object, Result, Type,
};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

fn contextual<T>(rule: &str, f: impl FnOnce() -> Result<T>) -> Result<T> {
    f().map_err(|e| e.context(rule))
}
fn context_key(key: &str) -> Result<(&str, u128)> {
    let (name, controls) = key
        .split_once(':')
        .ok_or_else(|| Error::new("§12: variant:inherited_controls key required".into()))?;
    variant(&Json::String(name.into()))?;
    let n = crate::object::host_count(controls, "inherited control count")?;
    Ok((name, n as u128))
}
/// The closed §8.1 AST; frame expressions bind only c.
pub(crate) fn expression(value: &Json, variables: &BTreeSet<&str>) -> Result<()> {
    crate::metadata_schema::validate("expression", value)?;
    match value {
        Json::Number(_) => {}
        Json::Object(fields) => {
            if fields.contains_key("var") {
                if !variables.contains(value.get("var")?.string()?) {
                    return err("§8.1", "expression references an unbound variable");
                }
            } else if fields.contains_key("op") {
                let arity = match value.get("op")?.string()? {
                    "+" | "-" | "*" | "/" | "max" | "min" => 2,
                    "ceil" => 1,
                    "log2inv_eps" => 0,
                    _ => return err("§8.1", "unknown expression operator"),
                };
                let args = value.get("args")?.array()?;
                if args.len() != arity {
                    return err("§8.1", "expression arity mismatch");
                }
                if value.get("op")?.string()? == "log2inv_eps" && !variables.contains("eps") {
                    return err("§8.1", "epsilon is unbound");
                }
                for a in args {
                    expression(a, variables)?;
                }
            } else {
                text(value.get("table")?)?;
                let index = value.get("index")?.string()?;
                if (index == "k" && !variables.contains("k"))
                    || (index == "eps_decade" && !variables.contains("eps"))
                    || !["k", "eps_decade"].contains(&index)
                {
                    return err("§8.1", "unbound table index");
                }
            }
        }
        _ => return err("§8.1", "expression must be a number or closed AST"),
    }
    Ok(())
}
fn frame(value: &Json) -> Result<()> {
    contextual("§9.4", || {
        for slot in value.get("interface")?.array()? {
            if slot.get("width")?.uint()? == 0 {
                return err("§9.4", "zero-width frame slot");
            }
        }
        let variables = BTreeSet::from(["c"]);
        expression(value.get("retained_history")?, &variables)?;
        for key in ["scratch", "borrow", "scheme_ancillae"] {
            for count in value.get(key)?.object()?.values() {
                if key == "scratch"
                    && count
                        .object()
                        .is_ok_and(|m| m.contains_key("clean") || m.contains_key("dirty"))
                {
                    expression(count.get("clean")?, &variables)?;
                    expression(count.get("dirty")?, &variables)?;
                } else {
                    expression(count, &variables)?;
                }
            }
        }
        if let Some(ranges) = value.object()?.get("live_ranges") {
            let mut grouped: BTreeMap<String, Vec<(u128, u128)>> = BTreeMap::new();
            for r in ranges.array()? {
                let id = text(r.get("frame")?)?.to_string();
                let lo = r.get("start")?.uint()?;
                let hi = r.get("end")?.uint()?;
                if lo >= hi {
                    return err("§9.4", "empty or inverted shared-frame live range");
                }
                let prior = grouped.entry(id).or_default();
                if prior.iter().any(|(a, b)| lo < *b && *a < hi) {
                    return err("§9.4", "shared frame has overlapping live ranges");
                }
                prior.push((lo, hi));
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    })
}
fn frame_width(v: &Json, c: u128) -> Result<u128> {
    // Evaluate with exact rational arithmetic; large intermediates report host
    // capacity rather than changing the declared width.
    fn eval(v: &Json, c: u128) -> Result<(i128, i128)> {
        let capacity =
            || crate::resource_error("§9.4", "frame expression exceeds rational host capacity");
        if matches!(v, Json::Number(_)) {
            let t = v.canonical();
            let (mantissa, exp) = t.split_once('e').map_or(Ok((t.as_str(), 0i32)), |(m, e)| {
                e.parse::<i32>().map(|e| (m, e)).map_err(|_| capacity())
            })?;
            let (whole, fractional) = mantissa.split_once('.').unwrap_or((mantissa, ""));
            let scale = (fractional.len() as i32)
                .checked_sub(exp)
                .ok_or_else(capacity)?;
            let mut num = format!("{whole}{fractional}")
                .parse::<i128>()
                .map_err(|_| capacity())?;
            let den = if scale >= 0 {
                10i128.checked_pow(scale as u32).ok_or_else(capacity)?
            } else {
                num = num
                    .checked_mul(
                        10i128
                            .checked_pow(scale.unsigned_abs())
                            .ok_or_else(capacity)?,
                    )
                    .ok_or_else(capacity)?;
                1
            };
            return Ok((num, den));

#[test]
fn native_phase_and_hadamard_full_basis_matrices() {
    let bytes = include_str!("../../spec/ref-0.9.json");
    let mut e = Expander::new(bytes, Registry::new()).unwrap();
    let q = Real::new(0, 1, 1);
    let mut count = 0;
    for width in 1..=4 {
        for g in ["h", "s", "sdg", "t", "tdg", "cz"] {
            if g == "cz" && width < 2 {
                continue;
            }
            let args = json::parse(&if g == "cz" {
                r#"["r[0]","r[1]","c"]"#.to_string()
            } else {
                r#"["r[0]","c"]"#.to_string()
            })
            .unwrap();
            let leaves = e
                .expand(
                    &format!("gate.{g}"),
                    args.as_arr().unwrap(),
                    &[("r", width), ("c", 1)],
                )
                .unwrap();
            let inputs = (0..width)
                .map(|i| ("r".into(), i))
                .chain([("c".into(), 0)])
                .collect::<Vec<_>>();
            for basis in 0..1 << (width + 1) {
                let mut expected = vec![Complex::default(); 1 << (width + 1)];
                if basis >> width & 1 == 0 {
                    expected[basis] = Complex::one();
                } else if g == "h" {
                    let a = Complex {
                        re: q,
                        im: Real::integer(0),
                    };
                    expected[basis & !1] = a;
                    expected[basis | 1] = if basis & 1 == 0 { a } else { a.negated() };
                } else {
                    let phase = if basis & 1 == 0 {
                        Complex::one()
                    } else {
                        match g {
                            "s" => Complex {
                                re: Real::integer(0),
                                im: Real::integer(1),
                            },
                            "sdg" => Complex {
                                re: Real::integer(0),
                                im: Real::integer(-1),
                            },
                            "t" => Complex { re: q, im: q },
                            "tdg" => Complex {
                                re: q,
                                im: Real::new(0, -1, 1),
                            },
                            "cz" => {
                                if basis & 2 != 0 {
                                    Complex::one().negated()
                                } else {
                                    Complex::one()
                                }
                            }
                            _ => unreachable!(),
                        }
                    };
                    expected[basis] = phase;
                }
                assert_eq!(column(&leaves, &inputs, basis).unwrap(), expected);
                count += 1;
            }
        }
    }
    assert_eq!(count, 356);
    println!("native Clifford+T: {count} exact matrix columns");
}
#[test]
fn variadic_mcx_full_domain_including_zero_and_large_arity() {
    use bennett_isa_conformance::{ref_bdd::Bdd, ref_catalog::Machine, ref_language::permutation};
    let bytes = include_str!("../../spec/ref-0.9.json");
    let mut e = Expander::new(bytes, Registry::new()).unwrap();
    let mut count = 0;
    for k in [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 17] {
        let args = json::parse(&format!(r#"[{k},"controls","target",[]]"#)).unwrap();
        let bundles = [("controls", k), ("target", 1)];
        let leaves = e
            .expand("gate.mcx", args.as_arr().unwrap(), &bundles)
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(leaves[0].wires.len(), k + 1);
        let m = Machine::compile(&permutation(&leaves).unwrap(), &bundles).unwrap();
        let mut b = Bdd::new();
        let mut s = m.symbolic_state();
        for name in ["controls", "target"] {
            for i in &m.inputs[name] {
                s[*i] = b.variable();
            }
        }
        let enabled = b.variable();
        let old = s.clone();
        let mut active = enabled;
        for i in &m.inputs["controls"] {
            active = b.and(active, old[*i]);
        }
        let mut expected = old.clone();
        let t = m.inputs["target"][0];
        expected[t] = b.xor(expected[t], active);
        m.run_symbolic(&mut b, &mut s, enabled, false, 1).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(s, expected);
        m.run_symbolic(&mut b, &mut s, enabled, true, 1).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(s, old);
        count += 1usize << (k + 2);
    }
    assert_eq!(count, 524412);
    println!("variadic mcx: {count} basis tuples");
}

fn binary64_angles_round_trip_and_numerical_adjoint_matrices() {
    let bytes = include_str!("../../spec/ref-0.9.json");

        0x3ff0000000000000,

                .expand(
                    &format!("gate.{g}"),
                    args.as_arr().unwrap(),
                    &[("r", 1), ("c", 1)],

                .unwrap();

                .expand("adj", body.as_arr().unwrap(), &[("r", 1)])
                .unwrap();

            let inverse = rotation(g, bits ^ (1 << 63)).unwrap();
            for (col, _) in matrix[0].iter().enumerate() {
                for (row, inverse_row) in inverse.iter().enumerate() {
                    let mut value = (0.0, 0.0);
                    for k in 0..2 {

                        let (br, bi) = matrix[k][col];
                        value.0 += ar * br - ai * bi;
                        value.1 += ar * bi + ai * br;

                    assert!((value.0 - expected).abs() <= 8.0 * f64::EPSILON);
                    assert!(value.1.abs() <= 8.0 * f64::EPSILON);
                }
            }
            // All computational basis columns with widths 1..4, including
            // spectator and effective-control bits: embed the prescribed matrix.
            for width in 1..=4 {
                for basis in 0..1 << (width + 1) {
